It's hard to describe, but it's almost like I'm watching myself from across the room instead of actually being there. It's been happening enough that I figured I needed to come in and talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt detached from yourself, like you're observing your own thoughts or body from outside, or felt like the world around you isn't real?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. That's pretty much what I was just trying to describe. It's exactly like that. I'll just be sitting at my desk working on tax returns, or even just sitting in my own house, and everything just feels... unreal. And yeah, it really does feel like I'm completely detached, just watching myself from across the room instead of actually being in my own body. So, yes, that is exactly what I've been experiencing.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of detachment or unreality ever occurred when you were under the influence of drugs or medications, or have they happened completely on their own without any clear trigger?\n\nPatient: No, I don't use any drugs or anything like that. And I'm not taking any medications that would cause something like this. It really just happens completely on its own. \n\nLike I said, I'll just be sitting there working, completely sober, and it just... hits me. There's really no clear trigger for it at all, it just comes out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of detachment or unreality happen more often when you're feeling stressed or anxious, or do they seem to occur independently of your mood or situation?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed a pattern, to be honest. I mean, being a tax preparer, there's always a baseline level of stress, but I wouldn't say I feel particularly anxious right before it happens. \n\nIt really just... seems to happen independently of my mood. I could be having a perfectly fine day, feeling totally normal, and then suddenly everything just feels off. So, no, I don't think it's tied to when I'm stressed. It just kind of does what it wants.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of detachment or unreality? Have they been happening for more than a year?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not a year. It's actually a pretty recent thing. This has only been going on for about two weeks. It all started right after this incident where a client got really angry and threatened me in my office. So, yeah, just the last couple of weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of detachment or unreality been happening every day, and have they lasted for at least a month total?\n\nPatient: No, not a month. Like I just mentioned, it's only been going on for about two weeks. It all started right after that client threatened me in my office, and it's been happening since then. But definitely not a month.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of detachment and unreality happening constantly throughout the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't exactly kept track of it like that. \n\nI mean... I don't think it's happening every single minute of the day, but I really haven't noticed if it completely goes away and comes back, or if it's just kind of lingering in the background. I just know it's been happening over these last two weeks, but I couldn't tell you for sure if it's constant or not.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
